Hi, nice to meet you too. Well, what finally pushed me to make an appointment is... work is just getting really difficult. I manage a health clinic, so I have to do all these EPIC quality reports. Which, by the way, EPIC has been an absolute nightmare since the system update last weekend. Our IT guy, Dave, swore it was going to make the billing codes easier to pull, but half the nursing staff got locked out on Monday morning and I spent three hours just on hold with the vendor. It's always something with the software, you know? My husband told me I shouldn't take my work stress home, but it's hard not to when the system is always crashing. 

But, um, anyway, the actual problem is that I can start one of those EPIC reports, or sit down in a clinic metrics meeting with every intention of tracking it, and... within just a few minutes, my mind has drifted so far that I’m just rereading the exact same numbers over and over. Oh, yeah, definitely. Even back in elementary school, I remember teachers always writing on my report cards that I was bright but careless. My mother hated that word. She actually kept all those old report cards in this big cedar chest at the foot of her bed. I still have that chest, actually in my guest room. When she passed away a few years ago, my sister really wanted it, but she lives in this tiny condo up in Chicago and there was just no way it was going to fit. Have you ever tried moving solid wood furniture into a Chicago walk-up? The stairwells are impossibly narrow. chores, or duties in the workplace.\n  manifestation: If someone gives me three or four follow-up steps after a committee meeting, I may do the first one and maybe draft the second, but then the email, spreadsheet, or final submission sits unfinished until someone reminds me.\n- key: S006\n  name: Sustained_Mental_Effort_Avoidance\n  description: Often avoids, dislikes, or is reluctant to ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, it really started getting noticeably worse about two years and four months ago. That's when I got promoted at the clinic. Before that, I had these little systems that worked fine, you know? But then they gave me this much larger portfolio to manage, and it just completely overwhelmed everything I used to rely on. 

It was right around the same time my sister bought her new house, actually. She moved out to the suburbs, which I told her was a mistake because the commute is just brutal. She spends at least an hour on the freeway every morning, and you know how traffic gets around here. Plus, she bought this older house that needs so much work. The plumbing is a nightmare. She had a pipe burst in the guest bathroom just a week after she moved in, and it flooded the whole hallway. They had to rip up the original hardwood floors, which was such a shame because they were gorgeous. Real oak, not that laminate stuff everyone uses now. I always wanted hardwood floors in our bedroom, but my husband says carpet is better for the winter. He gets cold feet at night, so he wears these thick wool socks to bed...

Oh, I'm sorry, I'm doing it again. But yeah, the focus issues... it was the promotion. Two years and four months ago. That's when it all really started falling apart for me.

No, I haven't really felt down or depressed like that. I mean, I get frustrated, obviously, with work and everything, but not sad. My mood is usually pretty okay. 

Actually, speaking of moods, my neighbor just got this new puppy, a golden retriever, and you cannot be sad around that dog. He is just the happiest little thing. But he barks constantly when she leaves for work. She works at the bank downtown, the one with the big clock tower? They just renovated the lobby there, and it looks so modern now, all glass and steel. I don't really like it, honestly. I prefer the old architecture, like the library on 4th street. They have those amazing stone lions out front. My grandfather used to take me there when I was little, and we would spend hours in the children's section... 

Um, but no, I'm not depressed. Just... scattered, I guess. But not down in the dumps.

Have you ever tried moving solid wood furniture into a Chicago walk-up? The stairwells are impossibly narrow. We ended up having to hire these specialized movers just to get her couch in, and it cost an absolute fortune. I told her she should just buy things flat-packed, but she loves antiques, so... \n\nWait, I'm sorry, what was the question? Oh, right, childhood focus. Yeah, so because of the whole focus thing, my mother actually had to sit with me at the kitchen table every single night while I finished my homework. If she didn't literally sit right there, I would just completely lose track of my assignments, or I'd realize I forgot my books at school and we'd have to go back and get them.\n\nDoctor: Do you notice this same trouble staying focused in other parts of your life besides work?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, definitely. Outside of work, I do a lot of... or I used to do a lot of volunteer health workshops in the community. We hold them at the rec center over on 4th street. Which, by the way, they just completely remodeled the lobby there and put in this incredible new espresso machine. It's one of those fancy Italian ones with the double boiler? My husband actually bought me a burr grinder for Christmas because he is absolutely obsessed with coffee. He orders these special beans from a tiny roaster in Seattle, and he insists that pre-ground coffee is the reason I always get heartburn. He’s probably right, honestly, but who has the time to manually grind beans every single morning when you're already running late? I mean, the noise alone wakes up our dog, and then she starts barking at the window, and it's just a whole ordeal before 7 AM... \n\nWait, I completely lost my train of thought. I'm sorry, what were we talking about? Oh! The workshops. Right. \n\nSo, yeah, I actually had to step back from leading parts of those volunteer workshops recently. I just can't reliably keep all the planning pieces moving anymore. I'd sit down to organize the schedule and just completely zone out. And honestly, it's getting really bad at the clinic, too. My delays are actually starting to affect our reporting deadlines. It’s awful because I'm making other teams wait on me to finish my part, and I just... I can't seem to stay focused long enough to get it done on time.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or like you need to keep moving, even when you’re supposed to sit still?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I can sit still just fine. It's my husband who's the restless one. He can never just sit and watch a movie with me. He's always getting up to check his phone, or puttering around in the kitchen, or deciding right in the middle of a scene that he absolutely has to organize the garage. Which, honestly, the garage is a total disaster area right now. We had all these boxes left over from when we redid the downstairs bathroom last fall. The contractor we used was terrible, just the worst. He promised it would take three weeks, and it ended up taking two months, and he left dust everywhere. I swear I'm still finding drywall dust on the baseboards. We should have just gone with the guy my sister recommended, but he was booked out until spring... \n\nBut, um, anyway, what was I saying? Oh, right, moving around. No, I don't really have that problem. I can sit in my chair for hours, it's just that my brain is a million miles away.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying excessively about many different things, even when there isn’t an immediate problem?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I worry all the time. I mean, I worry about normal things, like my husband's cholesterol or whether the dog is getting enough exercise. She's a rescue, you know, a mix of terrier and something else, maybe beagle? The shelter wasn't really sure. We got her about five years ago, right around the time my husband's mother moved into that assisted living place over in Westlake. That place is beautiful, honestly. They have a whole dining room that looks like a high-end restaurant, and they do these weekly outings to the symphony and the art museum. I always tell my husband I want to move in there when I'm older. The food is actually really good, too. They had this roasted salmon dish last time we visited that was perfectly cooked, which is so hard to do for a large group. I tried to recreate it at home, but I think my oven runs a little hot, because the edges got all dry...\n\nWait, sorry. Worrying. No, I don't think I'm an overly anxious person. I just get frustrated when I can't get my work done, but I don't sit around worrying about bad things happening or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: When did your trouble focusing start getting noticeably worse?\n\nPatient: Well, it really started getting noticeably worse about two years and four months ago. That's when I got promoted at the clinic. Before that, I had these little systems that worked fine, you know? But then they gave me this much larger portfolio to manage, and it just completely overwhelmed everything I used to rely on. \n\nIt was right around the same time my sister bought her new house, actually. She moved out to the suburbs, which I told her was a mistake because the commute is just brutal. She spends at least an hour on the freeway every morning, and you know how traffic gets around here. Plus, she bought this older house that needs so much work. The plumbing is a nightmare. She had a pipe burst in the guest bathroom just a week after she moved in, and it flooded the whole hallway. They had to rip up the original hardwood floors, which was such a shame because they were gorgeous. Real oak, not that laminate stuff everyone uses now. I always wanted hardwood floors in our bedroom, but my husband says carpet is better for the winter. He gets cold feet at night, so he wears these thick wool socks to bed...\n\nOh, I'm sorry, I'm doing it again. But yeah, the focus issues... it was the promotion. Two years and four months ago. That's when it all really started falling apart for me.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you felt down or depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really felt down or depressed like that. I mean, I get frustrated, obviously, with work and everything, but not sad. My mood is usually pretty okay. \n\nActually, speaking of moods, my neighbor just got this new puppy, a golden retriever, and you cannot be sad around that dog. He is just the happiest little thing. But he barks constantly when she leaves for work. She works at the bank downtown, the one with the big clock tower? They just renovated the lobby there, and it looks so modern now, all glass and steel. I don't really like it, honestly. I prefer the old architecture, like the library on 4th street. They have those amazing stone lions out front. My grandfather used to take me there when I was little, and we would spend hours in the children's section... \n\nUm, but no, I'm not depressed. Just... scattered, I guess. But not down in the dumps."

Subtypes: Psychomotor Agitation, Psychomotor Retardation (Motor), Psychomotor Retardation (Speech)\n[S029] Fatigue: Persistent fatigue that is not relieved by rest and a marked lack of energy.   }
}

Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health. Subtypes: Fear of Weight Gain, Fear of Specific Foods, Obsession with Weight Gain\n[S046] Body_Image_Weight_Shape_Disturbance: Undue influence of body weight or shape on self-evaluation.  Subtypes: Distorted Body Image, Self-Worth Dependent on Weight\n[S047] Compensatory_Behavior_NonPurging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors to prevent weight gain without purging (direct removal of food).  Subtypes: Fasting, Excessive Exercise\n[S048] Compensatory_Behavior_Purging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors involving the expulsion of food or fluids to prevent weight gain.  Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight.  Subtypes: Denial of Medical Risk, Disregard of Symptoms, Dismissal of Concerns\n[S056] Excessive_Anxiety_Worry: Excessive anxiety and worry (expectant apprehension) occurring persistently about a number of events or activities.  Subtypes: Chronic Worry, Generalized Worry, Catastrophizing, Intolerance of Uncertainty\n[S057] Uncontrollable_Worry: The subjective experience of finding it difficult to stop or control the worry.  Subtypes: Failure to Control, Intrusive Worry, Chaining of Worry\n[S058] Restlessness_Tension: Restlessness, or feeling keyed up or on edge.  Subtypes: Feeling Keyed Up, Physical Tension, Nervousness\n[S059] Concentration_Difficulty: Difficulty concentrating or mind going blank.  Subtypes: Interference by Worry, Inability to Sustain Attention, Mind Going Blank\n[S060] Irritability: A state of reacting sensitively or sharply to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Low Frustration Tolerance, Snappiness, Exhausted Patience\n[S061] Muscle_Tension: A state where muscles are continuously tense, stiff, and accompanied by discomfort.  Subtypes: Neck and Shoulder Tension, Jaw Tension, Generalized Muscle Aches, Chronic Stiffness\n[S062] Specific_Phobic_Fear: Marked fear or anxiety about a specific object or situation that is out of proportion to the actual danger. (Note: In children, may be expressed by crying, tantrums, freezing, or clinging).  Subtypes: Animal Type, Natural Environment Type, Blood-Injection-Injury Type, Situational Type, Other Type\n[S063] Immediate_Fear/Anxiety_Response: The phobic object or situation almost always provokes immediate fear or anxiety.  Subtypes: Anticipatory Anxiety, Immediate Panic Response, Physiological Panic Symptoms, Freezing\n[S064] Phobic_Avoidance_Endure: The phobic object or situation is actively avoided or endured with intense fear or anxiety.  Subtypes: Active Avoidance, Functional Impairment due to Avoidance, Enduring with Distress, Reliance on Safety Signals\n[S065] Obsessions: Recurrent and persistent thoughts, urges, or images that are experienced as intrusive and unwanted, and attempts to ignore or suppress them.  Subtypes: Contamination Obsessions, Pathological Doubt, Symmetry and Exactness Obsessions, Forbidden or Taboo Thoughts, Somatic Obsessions\n[S066] Compulsions: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to an obsession or to prevent anxiety/distress; clearly excessive or not realistically connected.  Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others.  Subtypes: Skin Defects, Facial Features, Asymmetry, Muscle Dysmorphia, Specific Body Parts\n[S068] Appearance_Related_Repetitive_Behaviors: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to appearance concerns.  Subtypes: Mirror Checking or Avoidance, Excessive Camouflaging, Skin Picking, Reassurance Seeking, Comparing, Seeking Cosmetic Procedures\n[S069] Intrusive_Symptoms: Recurrent, involuntary, and intrusive distressing memories of the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Internal Avoidance, External Avoidance\n[S071] Trauma_Related_Amnesia: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).  Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world.  Subtypes: Negative Self-Beliefs, Negative Beliefs about Others, Negative Worldview\n[S073] Distorted_Cognitions_With_Blame: Persistent, distorted cognitions about the cause or consequences of the traumatic event that lead the individual to blame himself/herself or others.  Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings).  Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression. Subtypes: With Depressed Mood, With Anxiety, With Mixed Anxiety and Depressed Mood, With Disturbance of Conduct, With Mixed Disturbance of Emotions and Conduct, Unspecified\n[S082] Depersonalization_Derealization: Altered sense of the reality of one's self or surroundings after a traumatic event.
